AI in Software Engineering - Assignment Book
AI in Software Engineering
Case Study Assignment Book
75 individual assignment sheets with case tasks, theory questions and lab questions 
covering all five modules
How to use this book
• Find your case number (given by your faculty, usually your roll number). Use only your own sheet.
• Each sheet has four parts: Part 1 case tasks (Tasks A to E, one per module), Part 2 theory questions 
(25), Part 3 lab questions (25) and Part 4 submission instructions.
• The 25 theory and 25 lab questions cover every topic of Module 1 to Module 5. Each module has 5 theory 
and 5 lab questions, tagged Basic, Applied or Advanced.
• Do the lab questions on a computer with one small prototype of your case, and keep the code in a GitHub 
repository.
• AI tools are allowed and expected. Record every use in the AI usage log, verify every answer, and add 
the AI disclosure statement.
• Answer in your own words. Every answer must refer to your case, not to a general example.
Module coverage of every sheet

Case 01 - Hospital OPD Appointment System
ASSIGNMENT SHEET - CASE 01
Case 01: Hospital OPD Appointment System
Student name Roll number
Class / section Submission date
Your Case
Scenario: A 200-bed hospital serves 1,500 OPD patients a day and loses about 35% of reception time on 
phone bookings. The health department has frozen the process rules for 6 months, but doctors change their 
availability every morning.
Process model (Module 
1)
Waterfall
Agile method (Module 
2)
Scrum (for post-launch changes)
AI tools to use ChatGPT and Copilot
Prototype module to 
build
slot booking with clash check
Do not change the items above. Use the numbers in the scenario as your targets in the tasks and labs.
Part 1 - Case Tasks (answer in this order)
Task A (Module 1 - Lifecycle and AI): Draw the Waterfall plan with a sign-off deliverable at each phase. Ask 
ChatGPT to turn 8 lines of doctor and reception interview notes into requirements and mark vague words such 
as 'urgent patient'. Explain where Waterfall hurts when schedules change daily.
Task B (Module 2 - Agile planning with AI): Turn 10 post-launch change requests into stories. Estimate them 
with planning poker and with ChatGPT, then compare. Assign Scrum roles to hospital staff (Product Owner = 
OPD manager).
Task C (Module 3 - Development and deployment): Use GitHub Flow (feature branch and pull request). 
Improve a Copilot prompt for the clash function over 3 versions. Jenkins runs build and unit tests; the Docker 
image reads the database path from an environment variable.
Task D (Module 4 - Testing and monitoring): Apply boundary value analysis to slot times (first, last, 
overlapping). Selenium test: book, reschedule, cancel. Ask AI for negative cases such as booking in the past. 
Monitor booking failure rate and page response time.
Task E (Module 5 - Quality and ethics): Reliability target: 99.5% successful bookings. Run SonarQube for 
duplication and complexity. Ethics: patient names and phone numbers must never be pasted into ChatGPT; 
show how you mask sample data.
Part 2 - Theory Questions (25 questions, all topics of all five modules)
Answer in your own words with diagrams or tables where asked. Every answer must refer to this case.
Module 1 - Software Engineering, SDLC and AI Tools
T1.1 [Basic] Define software engineering and explain why an engineered approach is needed for Hospital OPD 
Appointment System instead of ad-hoc coding. Name the SDLC phases and the main output of each.
T1.2 [Basic] For Hospital OPD Appointment System, rank all five process models from best fit to worst fit and 
justify the top and the bottom choice.
T1.3 [Applied] Differentiate the AI-driven development lifecycle from the traditional SDLC with a phase-wise 
table. Mention one human checkpoint for each phase in Hospital OPD Appointment System.
T1.4 [Applied] Explain how Copilot, Tabnine and ChatGPT differ in the way they generate code. What data-
privacy question must be asked before using each tool on Hospital OPD Appointment System?
T1.5 [Advanced] Discuss the limits of AI-assisted requirement analysis when the stakeholders of Hospital OPD 
Appointment System give vague or conflicting inputs. How should a software engineer respond?
Page 4
Case 01 - Hospital OPD Appointment System
Module 2 - Agile Practices and AI-Driven Planning
T2.1 [Basic] State the four values and any six principles of the Agile Manifesto and show how each applies to 
Hospital OPD Appointment System.
T2.2 [Basic] Explain Scrum in detail and list its practices. State one limitation when it is applied to Hospital OPD 
Appointment System.
T2.3 [Applied] Explain Product Backlog, Sprint Backlog, Increment and Definition of Done, giving an example 
of each for Hospital OPD Appointment System.
T2.4 [Applied] Explain how AI can assist backlog creation, effort estimation and scheduling. What data would 
an AI need to estimate stories for Hospital OPD Appointment System reliably?
T2.5 [Advanced] Discuss the governance rules needed when a team uses AI tools in sprint planning and 
retrospectives for Hospital OPD Appointment System, including privacy of meeting content.
Module 3 - Development and Deployment Practices using AI Tools
T3.1 [Basic] List the stages of development where AI tools can be used for Hospital OPD Appointment System 
and the human check required at each stage.
T3.2 [Applied] What is prompt engineering? Explain the parts of a good prompt and write a weak and a strong 
prompt for slot booking with clash check.
T3.3 [Basic] Compare GitHub Flow, GitFlow and trunk-based development. Which suits Hospital OPD 
Appointment System and why?
T3.4 [Applied] Describe how a CI/CD pipeline reduces risk for Hospital OPD Appointment System. Mention one 
risk it does not remove.
T3.5 [Advanced] Explain Docker image, container, Dockerfile and Docker Compose. Why is containerisation 
useful for Hospital OPD Appointment System?
Module 4 - AI-Based Testing and Monitoring
T4.1 [Basic] Explain verification versus validation, and bug versus failure, with examples from Hospital OPD 
Appointment System.
T4.2 [Basic] Which defects in Hospital OPD Appointment System would appear only in integration or system 
testing and not in unit testing? Give two examples.
T4.3 [Applied] What is Selenium? Explain WebDriver, locators, waits and assertions. Which user flow of 
Hospital OPD Appointment System would you automate first and why?
T4.4 [Applied] List AI tools for testing and monitoring. What are the risks of accepting AI-generated tests for 
Hospital OPD Appointment System without review?
T4.5 [Advanced] Discuss benefits and challenges of AI in software testing for Hospital OPD Appointment 
System, including false positives and trust.
Module 5 - AI-Powered Quality Assurance and Ethical Development
T5.1 [Basic] Define Software Quality Assurance. Explain correctness, reliability, maintainability, usability and 
efficiency, with one measurable target of each for Hospital OPD Appointment System.
T5.2 [Applied] Explain how a quality gate would be added to the CI pipeline of Hospital OPD Appointment 
System, and which conditions you would set.
T5.3 [Applied] Prepare a checklist for safely reviewing AI-generated code in Hospital OPD Appointment 
System.
T5.4 [Advanced] Explain responsible AI principles (fairness, transparency, accountability, privacy, safety) and 
apply them to Hospital OPD Appointment System.
T5.5 [Advanced] What data must never be shared with an AI tool in a project like Hospital OPD Appointment 
System? Explain safe practices with examples.
Part 3 - Lab Questions (25 practical exercises, all topics of all five modules)
Do each exercise on a computer, using one small prototype of the case. Submit the evidence named in each question 
(code, screenshots with captions, tables, logs).
Module 1 Labs - SDLC, Process Models and AI Tools
L1.1 [Basic] Make a one-page SDLC plan for Hospital OPD Appointment System listing phase, activity, output 
and owner. Submit: the plan.
Page 5
Case 01 - Hospital OPD Appointment System
L1.2 [Applied] Build a weighted decision matrix of the five process models for Hospital OPD Appointment 
System using 5 criteria that matter in the scenario. Submit: matrix and a 5-line conclusion that supports 
Waterfall.
L1.3 [Applied] Write the main function of slot booking with clash check by hand first, then ask AI to improve it. 
List every change and whether it was valid. Submit: before and after code and a change log.
L1.4 [Applied] Use AI to convert the scenario of Hospital OPD Appointment System into 10 functional and 5 
non-functional requirements. Rewrite them to be testable. Submit: before and after table.
L1.5 [Advanced] Ask AI to generate test cases for slot booking with clash check. Add the boundary and 
negative cases it missed and compute the AI accuracy. Submit: table with origin (AI or you) and valid or invalid.
Module 2 Labs - Agile, Scrum and AI-Driven Planning
L2.1 [Basic] Create a one-page Agile working agreement for the Hospital OPD Appointment System team 
(communication, meetings, Definition of Done, change handling). Submit: the agreement.
L2.2 [Basic] Set up the Scrum framework for Hospital OPD Appointment System: roles or practices, events or 
board, and artifacts. Submit: one-page framework sheet with a name against each role.
L2.3 [Applied] Split one large epic of Hospital OPD Appointment System into 5 smaller stories. Submit: epic, 
stories and acceptance criteria.
L2.4 [Applied] Using velocity data that you assume for 3 past sprints, forecast the sprints needed for the 
backlog of Hospital OPD Appointment System and draw a burn-down. Submit: spreadsheet and chart.
L2.5 [Advanced] Use ChatGPT or Jira AI to summarise 10 tickets of Hospital OPD Appointment System and 
detect duplicates. Count the correct results. Submit: tickets, AI output and accuracy.
Module 3 Labs - Prompting, Git, Jenkins and Docker
L3.1 [Basic] Use role, few-shot and step-by-step prompting on slot booking with clash check and compare the 
results. Submit: three prompts and a comparison.
L3.2 [Applied] Build the working prototype module (slot booking with clash check) using ChatGPT and Copilot 
and by hand where needed. Record accepted and rejected suggestions with reasons. Submit: code and 
suggestion log.
L3.3 [Basic] Set branch protection on main and show a pull request that is blocked until a review passes. 
Submit: screenshots.
L3.4 [Applied] Set a Jenkins trigger so that every push to the repository builds slot booking with clash check. 
Submit: configuration and two build results.
L3.5 [Advanced] Write a Dockerfile for slot booking with clash check, build the image, run the container and 
show the application working. Explain every line. Submit: Dockerfile, commands and screenshot.
Module 4 Labs - Testing, Selenium and Monitoring
L4.1 [Basic] Perform one 20-minute exploratory testing session on slot booking with clash check and log what 
you found. Submit: session notes.
L4.2 [Basic] Write 8 unit tests for slot booking with clash check with PyTest or JUnit and show the coverage 
report. Submit: test code and report.
L4.3 [Applied] Run the same 10 test cases manually and with Selenium 5 times each and compare the time. 
Submit: time table and break-even estimate.
L4.4 [Applied] Score 10 tests of Hospital OPD Appointment System by likelihood and impact, rank them, and 
choose the top 5 to run first. Submit: scoring sheet.
L4.5 [Advanced] Write a script that checks a URL or function of Hospital OPD Appointment System every 
minute, logs the response time and raises an alert above a limit; run it for 10 minutes. Submit: script, log and 
chart.
Module 5 Labs - SQA, SonarQube and Ethical AI
L5.1 [Basic] Measure correctness (defect density) and reliability (failure rate over 50 runs) of slot booking with 
clash check. Submit: data and calculation.
L5.2 [Applied] Run SonarQube on slot booking with clash check; fix at least 5 issues and rescan. Submit: 
before and after dashboard screenshots and a table.
L5.3 [Applied] Write a safe-use checklist for AI code review in Hospital OPD Appointment System and apply it 
to one real AI suggestion. Submit: checklist and example.
Page 6
Case 01 - Hospital OPD Appointment System
L5.4 [Advanced] Let AI generate code and look for a deliberate weakness such as missing input validation; find 
and fix it. Submit: finding, fix and test.
L5.5 [Advanced] Test an AI feature or prompt related to Hospital OPD Appointment System with different 
names, regions or genders and check for unfair differences. Submit: inputs, outputs and mitigation.
Part 4 - Submission Instructions
• Submit one PDF or DOCX named Case01_RollNumber_Name.
• Order of the file: cover with case details, Part 1 answers, Part 2 answers (numbered T1.1 to T5.5), Part 3 
answers (numbered L1.1 to L5.5), then the appendices below.
• Keep the prototype (slot booking with clash check) in one GitHub repository and give the link on the cover 
page. Do not upload secrets or personal data.
• Use the same project name and the same IDs (FR-01, US-01, TC-01) in every part so that your answers 
connect.
• Use AI tools as the case requires, but record every use in the AI usage log, verify every output, and be 
ready to explain your work in an interview.

Case 02 - Core Banking Interest Engine
ASSIGNMENT SHEET - CASE 02
Case 02: Core Banking Interest Engine
Student name Roll number
Class / section Submission date
Your Case
Scenario: A cooperative bank computes daily savings interest for 80,000 accounts. A 1-paisa rounding error 
per account costs Rs. 800 a day, and rules differ for senior citizens, minimum balance and leap years.
Process model (Module 
1)
V-Model
Agile method (Module 
2)
XP
AI tools to use Tabnine (private repository)
Prototype module to 
build
daily interest calculation
Do not change the items above. Use the numbers in the scenario as your targets in the tasks and labs.
Part 1 - Case Tasks (answer in this order)
Task A (Module 1 - Lifecycle and AI): Draw the V-Model pairing each design level with a test level. Write a 
requirement-to-test mapping for 6 interest rules. Use Tabnine to complete the calculation and record which 
suggestions broke the rounding rule.
Task B (Module 2 - Agile planning with AI): Apply XP: 5 stories, test-first coding with a pair (human or AI), 
and one refactoring. Estimate in ideal days and compare with an AI estimate.
Task C (Module 3 - Development and deployment): Trunk-based Git with short-lived branches. Prompt for 
compound interest using decimal arithmetic, not floating point. Jenkins nightly scheduled job runs the 
calculation on sample data; Docker multi-stage build keeps the image small.
Task D (Module 4 - Testing and monitoring): Decision-table tests for age x balance x leap year. Integration 
test between the interest module and a ledger stub. Rank modules by defect risk using a change-count and 
past-defect table you create.
Task E (Module 5 - Quality and ethics): Correctness is the top attribute: count failed rule tests. Set a 
SonarQube quality gate on new code. Ethics: who is accountable if an AI-written formula mis-pays customers? 
Record the human sign-off step.
Part 2 - Theory Questions (25 questions, all topics of all five modules)
Answer in your own words with diagrams or tables where asked. Every answer must refer to this case.
Module 1 - Software Engineering, SDLC and AI Tools
T1.1 [Basic] Draw and explain the SDLC for Core Banking Interest Engine. Which phase is the most expensive 
to correct late, and why?
T1.2 [Basic] Compare Waterfall, Incremental, Spiral, Prototype and V-Model in a table (idea, strength, 
weakness, best use). Then explain why V-Model is the given model for Core Banking Interest Engine.
T1.3 [Applied] Explain how an AI development lifecycle (data, training, evaluation, deployment, monitoring) 
differs from the traditional SDLC, using Core Banking Interest Engine as the example.
T1.4 [Applied] A team building Core Banking Interest Engine must pick one AI assistant for daily interest 
calculation. Prepare a short evaluation covering accuracy, privacy, cost and learning effort.
T1.5 [Advanced] Explain how AI can support requirement analysis, code generation and test case generation. 
For Core Banking Interest Engine, give one benefit and one risk for each.

T4.5 [Advanced] What is continuous monitoring? List metrics, logs and alerts for Core Banking Interest Engine,

T5.3 [Applied] Explain AI-assisted code review. What can AI find in daily interest calculation and what can it 
miss or invent?
T5.4 [Advanced] Identify the main ethical risks of using AI to build Core Banking Interest Engine. How would 
you reduce each?
T5.5 [Advanced] Explain licensing and intellectual-property risks of AI-generated code and how you would 
manage them in Core Banking Interest Engine.

Case 03: Food Delivery MVP

Your Case
Scenario: A start-up has 4 weeks and 3 developers to show investors ordering from 10 restaurants. Investors 
will judge speed of ordering and how polished it feels.
Process model (Module 
1)
Prototype
Agile method (Module 
2)
Kanban
AI tools to use ChatGPT
Prototype module to 
build
cart and order placement
Do not change the items above. Use the numbers in the scenario as your targets in the tasks and labs.
Part 1 - Case Tasks (answer in this order)
Task A (Module 1 - Lifecycle and AI): Plan two prototype iterations (clickable screens, then a working order 
flow). Ask ChatGPT for 10 questions to ask 5 test users and summarise their answers. State what code is 
thrown away and what is kept.

pinned to a fixed base version for reproducibility.

with tolerance checks. AI proposes edge values for altitude limits. Monitor simulator error counts.
Task E (Module 5 - Quality and ethics): Targets: 0 critical failed tests; conversion error under 0.01%. 
SonarQube bug and security rules. Ethics: AI-generated safety code needs human certification; record who 
verified it.
